# LEDGER — frozen handoff history (through #804)

Contains: the old HANDOFF.md content that is history, moved verbatim at #805: the dated status ledger, the s6 "State after" blocks, s7 What remains, the pre-#805 s9 (with its new-chat prompt) and s10 Where we are.
Read when: only to look up an old decision or receipt; grep by #NNN or heading, never read whole, never append. Token cost: see STATE.md.

Text below is verbatim. Statements in it about "this file", "both handoff files", the freshness check or the new-chat prompt describe the process before #805 and are superseded by HANDOFF.md and RUNBOOK.md s9.

M2 NO-SAFE-CANDIDATE PASS-THROUGH - msc-6 t3491 / t3493 / t3495 have deepen > 0.5px and NO pick at all, so the controller declines and the original spin-opposed nudge applies (deepen 2.277 / 2.230 / 0.772); t3491 and t3495 are the two flail/dagger NEW visible ticks. M3 MIRROR-CLASS TRAJECTORY ARTIFACT - c-19 t11795 has ZERO events in t11793-11797: no resolver involvement, consistent with #770's finding that no mirror-class tick follows a deepening first nudge. THE DEGRADATION PEAK IS TRANSLATION, NOT ROTATION: the two worst ticks (t6446 7.451px, t6447 13.504px) have NO event at all - the resolver was progressing, so the stall/nudge branch never fired - and the pair goes 13.504 -> 7.226 by TRANSLATING; msc-6 carries 138 raw >5px pop records against base-6's 21 on FEWER fight ticks. CROSS-VARIANT PROOF that M1 is episode-intrinsic, not chaos: c-23 t8814-8817 and msc-23 t8443-8446 carry IDENTICAL pre / gOrig / deepen / pickG to full float precision, the same flip-both picks and the same two visible depths, 371 ticks apart in two different variants. THE CONSTRAINT LIST (C1-C6) and the conclusion - NO FURTHER SIGN-CHOICE VARIANT SHOULD BE BUILT - are in s7. See HANDOFF s7 (THE NEW-OVERLAP MECHANISM DIAGNOSIS) and BATCH3 #774. THE FIX (two measure.mjs edits, no canonical change): (A) the 3s pass reads a PRE-TICK entry-state flag snapshot (entryShotFlags, captured before tick(G) at :633) instead of post-tick G.shots — a read-instant semantics fix, proven by round-3; (B) the pair exemption gains two clauses — xor-returns (a one-sided returning shot's pivot disc is not its hitbox, the drawn arc is, per chaosorbit-r54 / snapfix-r57 — the same doctrine body/blade/rim use to exempt returns) and annihilation-event-frame (either shot departed this tick per shotGoneTick; for shot-shot departure IS the resolution, so this can never hide a live pass-through). Residual = two ALIVE non-returning enemy shots overlapping (the genuine tripwire target; none today). Measured on flail+hammer, flail+scythe and nunchaku+scythe, seeds 101-103, 10,000 ticks: 84 grown-chain hits, 0 phantoms with lenStep (36 without), 165 parries, 0 without touch with it (47 without); REPORTED, not asserted: 24 hits that ADDED a link (9 still read as phantoms: the event stores the post-grow pose, the contact used the pre-grow one) and 1 parry in a grow tick (1 without touch). Within a tick the stash is the ENTRY lenStep, so a second hit in the same tick, and a substep after an in-tick grow, are not exact either. Exactness at those ticks needs lenStep on the audit tape or in the events = a CANONICAL edit (rule 5, the owner's). THE OWNER'S RE-RUN ON THE lenStep FIX (2026-11-15; cmd.exe, plain > redirects, exit=0, 657.2s wall, 109 battles, 8 workers, canonical md5 unchanged): law 1 111 -> 12 phantom hits, law 2 117 -> 3 parries-without-touch, law 2s 116 -> 43 swats-without-touch, law 4 283 -> 66 replica mismatches and 123,260 -> 94,476 stuck flags; law 3 worst 27.399px -> 26.100px (natural seed 27 t4942, axe vs wand, non-chain) visible 614 -> 453; unchanged law 1s 495/2,849, law 3s 74.971px, law 4s 60, law 5 10, stall 5.26s. THE DEFECT-TRACKER RULING (owner 2026-11-15): contact-exactness and solidity are hard product requirements - a viewer must never see weapons tunnel through each other or damage/parry/swat fire without visible contact; the 6px and 20.754px calibrations are RETIRED; law 3's row becomes a defect tracker; open defects D1 infeasible pin (26.100px), D2 phantom shot hits (495), D3 whiff-swats (43), D4 visible shot-render overlaps; sequence (a) read-only probe of 1s/2s, (b) read-only rotational-DOF design probe for D1, (c) canonical fixes one at a time with re-baseline, (d) balance/trait pass. SEQUENCE ITEM (a) CLOSED (2026-11-15, read-only diagnostic, throwaway scratch-measure.mjs, wand+bow 8 seeds, 50 battles, owner-run in cmd.exe): the first 10 logged phantom shot hits and 10 whiff-swats all show the sim fired the event correctly (its substep contact test saw the overlap) while the event stores the shot's POST-SUBSTEP position, so recomputes from stored fields give false positives (logged gaps 0.05px to 52.20px; population worst 54.08px). D2 and D3 are therefore AUDIT artifacts (same family as the lenStep drift #757), NOT product defects - the viewer never sees damage or a swat without contact. Reclassified as disclosed audit artifacts; for a game that keeps adding weapons a hard 0-phantom shot law would chase audit artifacts forever. D1 and D4 remain the open defects. SEQUENCE ITEM (b) OPEN-LOOP RESULT (read-only throwaway scratch/rotdof-diag.mjs rev 5, owner-run, NOT committed; seed 27 natural, axe vs wand round 1): the anchor reproduces - deepest overlap 26.100px at OUR t4941 = measure's t4942 (offset -1: we capture the pose before tick(), measure labels it after; measure's t is absolute from game start, so a fixed window must not assume round-local t). 119 overlap ticks in the battle. The +/-0.40 rad rotation grid (bodies fixed, wAngle free, geom.parryGap - the same fn the sim's bladeVsBlade feeds at :2210-2211) clears ALL 5 deepest pins with a FINITE rotation: min-clear radii 0.18 / 0.30 / 0.26 / 0.14 / 0.14 rad (worst 0.30 at t5020), reaching 12.4-17.3px of real separation (gains ~36-41px). 1421 grid cells returned NON-FINITE parryGap - a degeneracy a real controller must cap below (the 0.14-0.30 clearances are interior/finite; boundary cells are not safe targets). CONCLUSION: D1 is a MISSING-ROTATIONAL-DOF defect, NOT a geometric hard limit - the fix direction is proven open-loop. At ~120 Hz and buzzsaw spin 12.6 rad/s, 0.30 rad is ~3 ticks of rotation, and the pin persists ~119 ticks, so the budget exists; contiguity per pin is unmeasured (the closed-loop sim is what confirms it). This is an OPEN-LOOP optimistic bound: the real nudge caps rotation (nudgePx/REACH, gated one/tick/fighter), so the controller must fit that budget - proven viable, not yet sized. RESOLVER LOCATION CORRECTION: the law-3 band-dump comment's line numbers are STALE. It says torque at sim :1697-98 and Math.sign at :1713, but :1697-1713 land in rollWeapons/startFight (f.wAngle=0 / f.wAngle=a), NOT the resolver. The real resolver is bladeVsBlade at :2206 (pose fill _pA/_pB :2210-2211 -> geom.parryGap; entry angles eaa/eba :2223; separateBodiesPos :2197). The words "torque" and "Math.sign" do NOT appear in the first 50 grep hits up to :2223. The resolver BODY (:2206 onward) is UNREAD - that is the next read-only step, and the comment's torque/Math.sign description is unverified against current code. SEQUENCE STATUS (#762): (b) OPEN-LOOP DONE - read bladeVsBlade's body (:2206+) and design the capped controller, then (b2) canonical = rule-5 owner decision; D1 stays OPEN (now with a proven fix direction), D2/D3 stay closed as audit artifacts (#760), D4 (law 3s visible subset) stays open and unprobed. RESOLVER BODY READ (#763, read-only, 2026-11-15, canonical md5 5de162de46eb4fa3204e3ee636f2aff3; the line numbers hold at that md5 only): bladeVsBlade (:2206) runs once per SUBSTEP (call site :1851, after wAngle += spin*chillMul*bloomMul*sdt at :1845). The resolver runs only when egap < 0: up to 16 passes of translation-only sequential impulses (0.4 x overlap per contact pair; loop body :2247+), then separateBodiesPos and clampToArenaPos; no angular state enters that loop. tqA/tqB (:2247, :2265-66) are per-call sums of lever x normal over penetrating circle pairs, used ONLY to pick the nudge sign - so the band-dump comment's torque/Math.sign description is CONFIRMED present (Math.sign at :2295-96); only its line numbers were stale. The nudge writes f.wAngle at exactly two sites, :2306 (a) and :2307 (b). Trigger: a stall (deep - gapPrev < 0.02) with deep < -1, at most once per call (nudged, :2277-78). Gate: one add per tick per fighter (nudgeTick !== G.t; reset to -1 at :1706 on a new round). Magnitude: min(0.6, 0.15 + 0.02*|deep|, CFG.nudgePx / REACH[weapon]) with nudgePx 12 (:113); the REACH values for axe and wand were NOT read. Sign: when latched (G.clash) it is spinSign * k with BIND_UNWIND true (:1260, so k = -1), i.e. SPIN-OPPOSED; unlatched it is Math.sign(torque) with spinSign as the tie-break. HYPOTHESES, NOT VERIFIED: (1) if the 26.100px anchor was latched, the spin-opposed nudge fights the orbit (12.6 rad/s at 120 Hz is ~0.105 rad per tick against a cap of 12/REACH) and can net to ~zero - the freeze bindfix-r35's own comment describes (:2287-90); (2) the open-loop grid reports clearance radii, not the signed direction, so the sign each pin needs is unmeasured, and whether G.clash was true at the anchor ticks is unmeasured. CONTROLLER SKETCH (a proposal only, NOT approved): keep the magnitude formula and the one-per-tick gate; in the nudge branch only, pick each fighter's sign by evaluating parryGap at wAngle +/- the nudge and taking the direction with the larger FINITE gap (fall back to today's rule if neither improves or both are non-finite); cap cumulative rotation per episode inside the finite interior (open-loop worst 0.30 rad, with margin) so no pose reaches the non-finite boundary; deterministic (no RNG), so the fingerprint moves only in cells where the nudge fires. The closed-loop proof (b2) would be a new .qa/rotdof-proof.mjs asserting the 5 anchors clear and no non-finite pose is reached. D1 EVIDENCE CORRECTION (#764, read-only, 2026-11-15; agent-run headless on the canonical md5 5de162de46eb4fa3204e3ee636f2aff3, NOT measure.mjs; scratch scripts, not committed; method: newGame(seed) with no forced weapons, tick(G) loop, the pose captured BEFORE each tick, geom.parryGap with the pre-tick lenStep, plus figT read at tick entry and hooks injected through loadSim's transform option at the nudge sites and at the egap line of bladeVsBlade). (1) The harness REPRODUCES the anchor: seed 27 natural, axe vs wand round 1, deepest overlap 26.100px at G.t 4941 (t5020 25.517px second), 119 overlap ticks, spins 6.5 / -12.6, G.clash false at entry and end of the top-8 ticks. (2) THE ANCHOR PAIR IS BLOOM-TRANSFIGURED: at t4941 the axe's figT is 670 and at t5020 591 (the wand's is 0) - the wand's bloom (bloom-r73) turned the AXE into a joke object. The sim deliberately makes such a pair solid-free: bladeVsBlade returns at :2209 on figT > 0 (comment: a joke object parries nothing and is parried by nothing; the pair goes solid-free: no latch, no clinch), so the resolver NEVER RAN at the anchor ticks: in the window G.t 4938-4946 the hook on the egap line logged zero calls, against 71,720 calls elsewhere in 5,300 ticks (470 with egap < 0). 93 of the 119 overlap ticks have figT > 0 on a fighter; the other 26 are at most 0.22px. (3) The audit computes the gap from the REAL axe geometry while the drawn weapon is the joke object (:3718) - measure.mjs and LAWS.md contain no bloom/figT/joke rule (grep -i, no hit). - THE LAW-3/3s VERDICT RULING - A DEFECT TRACKER (#768, 2026-11-16; harness-side, the canonical is untouched). RULING (the owner delegated it, "you do what you think is best for the project"; the previous agent ruled and the owner accepted): measure.mjs's law-3 and law-3s verdict lines become a DEFECT TRACKER with three states. PASS = the goal is met (law 3: 0 band-visible ticks; 3s: 0 blade+body+rim+pair). FAIL = a REGRESSION of any tracked counter vs the recorded baseline, checked BEFORE the goal, so a regression can never read as a pass. TRACK = otherwise (an open defect at or below baseline). Goal 0 stays the hard product requirement; the baseline is a regression TRIPWIRE, NOT an acceptance band. BASELINE = the owner's 2026-11-16 --solidity run on 3b11f34 (recorded in #766): law 3 band visible 42, band worst 14.185px, mirror 27 overlaps / 10 visible / worst 11.559px; law 3s blade 1,296 / body 19,609 / rim 54,605 / pair 1,061,228 / worst 74.971px. WHAT MOVED: only the two verdict expressions, their labels and the header tier comment (the retired 6px / 2% gate on law 3 and the zero-count / <1px gate on 3s are gone from those two lines; pf() still gates every other law). polyTicks and every counter computation are unchanged (diff review); the bloom counters (bloomOverlap / bloomVis / bloomWorst / shotRenderBladeBloom) stay on the [----] exemption line and are NOT ratcheted (#765). NOT MOVED: docs/LAWS.md (the law's text is the owner's) and frame-proof's C3 envelope gate; the new labels keep the exact anchors the LAWS.md C3 and S3 rows cite, which lint-docs checks (the first build of this edit dropped them and verify --quick caught it). IMPLEMENTATION: LAW3_BASE / LAW3S_BASE / VEPS / verdict3 / verdict3s sit between VERDICT-BLOCK markers after the f2 helper so .qa/verdict-proof.mjs can lift and run them; the call sites pass polyOverlapVis - polyFlipVis, polyBandWorst, polyFlipOverlap, polyFlipVis, polyFlipWorst (law 3) and shotRenderBlade / Body / Rim / Pair / Worst (3s). FLAGGED DEVIATION from the delegated text ("px floats carry a 1e-6 epsilon"): a px value is compared at the 3-decimal precision the baseline was recorded at (the report prints toFixed(3)) AND with the 1e-6 epsilon, and a non-finite value fails closed. Reason: the baseline figures are rounded, so a raw compare could call a value that rounds to the baseline a regression; the cost is that a px regression smaller than 0.0005 is invisible, which is below the baseline's own resolution. To go back to a raw compare, delete the r3() call in worsePx (one line). PROOF: .qa/verdict-proof.mjs 4/4 (V1 sites by source incl. retired-gate removal, regression-before-goal ordering, pf() still gating the other laws, bloom not ratcheted, the LAWS anchors present, polyTicks still counted; V2 21 enumerated cases on the lifted functions; V3 baseline pins to the numbers above; CONTROL 6/6 mutated copies caught). measure.mjs was NOT run: the owner's next --solidity run is the first exercise, and [TRACK]/[TRACK] with counters identical to the #766 report is a PREDICTION, not a measurement.

none ran 16), and the last call's post-resolver gap equals the tick-entry overlap to 3 decimals; (2) the first nudge in the tick - always latched there, spin-opposed (BIND_UNWIND, :2294-2296) - deepened the gap by its rotation alone in all 10 distinct events; none of the 78 clears within its call vs 82 of the other 87; on the same pre-nudge pose flipping both signs clears 51 and improves 54, the best of {flip A, flip B, flip both} clears 54, and for 20 of the 78 none improves on holding wAngle; a rebuilt +/-0.40 rad grid on seed 27's five deepest poses has 8,405 cells and 1,421 non-finite (#762's count), all clear (min 4.093px). #762 is superseded on this point, not rewritten; a controller must treat Infinity as the BEST outcome, and the nudge magnitude cap (nudgePx / REACH) remains a separate constraint; - THE VARIANT C TRIAL (#772, 2026-11-16; the owner's "go"; agent-run headless, read-only, throwaway scratch scripts scratch/c-lib.mjs (transform + hook), c-run.mjs (tick-entry measurement per seed), c-report.mjs, c-replay.mjs (one-tick replay), c-bake.mjs (scratch HTML copies with the hook baked in), runall.sh - gitignored, NOT in the repo, NOT measure.mjs; canonical md5 5de162de46eb4fa3204e3ee636f2aff3 checked before and after, tree clean). METHOD: loadSim's transform with 5 content anchors, each asserted to occur exactly once: the two sign constants (const sa / const sb become let and a hook call follows the sb statement), the two nudgeTick gate conditions (each gains `&& !__H.hold`, so a held call never consumes the once-per-tick gate), and `function bladeVsBlade(G) {` (renamed __bvbOrig; a wrapper logs how far the pair moves in one call). The hook calls parryGap with each candidate rotation at the current pre-nudge pose (positions as of that resolver pass; rotation alone) and saves/restores the shared _pg fields around every call. PROOF OF THE HOOK: plain sim vs hooked base in lockstep, a whole-state signature compared every tick over seeds 1-40 + forced flail+hammer s101: 519,189 ticks, 0 mismatches (the record-only hook, mode base, leaves the sim untouched). The tick-entry loop is the .qa/bloom-proof.mjs loop (pose captured before each tick: pos, wAngle, weapon, spin, fl.u, lenStep, alive, figT; leadFlip with the entry spin; overlap = gap < 0, visible = depth > 2.5px; bloom = figT > 0 at entry; mirror class = the bloom-proof prevParry/prevSub/STR test). CONVENTIONS: overlaps are keyed by the TICK-ENTRY t; the hook's event and pop t is the sim's G.t inside the tick = tick-entry t + 1 (the prior-agent's s24 t7617 pop is this file's t7618, 65.79px); measure's t = tick-entry t + 1. VARIANT C: keep the original rule's signs unless they deepen the one-step gap by more than 0.5px; then take the best of {hold, flip both, flip A, flip B} (NaN never wins, Infinity is best; a flip must beat hold by more than 1e-9 to be chosen, ties among flips go to flip-both, then A, then B); hold skips the nudge for BOTH fighters and does NOT consume nudgeTick. Hold is pair-wide here; the prior-agent's argmax was per-fighter {+,-,hold} (a method difference). BASE (same hook, seeds 1-40 natural): 465,772 fight ticks; 923 overlap, 291 bloom, 274 visible = 261 bloom-visible + 13 non-bloom (8 mirror + 5 neither); 165 first-nudge events, 78 deepening (latched 76/133, unlatched 2/32; 74 deepen by more than 0.5px - the prior-agent's 74 of 78 is CONFIRMED); of the 74, a flip beats hold in 55 and none does in 19 (the prior-agent counted 54 and 20; definitions may differ); longest latched-entry run 39 (s23, ends t4525). Forced s101 base: 17,537 fight ticks, 120 overlaps, 2 non-bloom visible (both neither), 68 events, 35 deepening. Pops (resolver calls whose body-separation change exceeds 20 / 50px): naturals 12 / 3 (s24 t7618 65.79px, s6 t6619 56.49px, s24 t9121 54.64px; max single-body move >20px: 11 calls, >50px: 0), s101 1 / 1 (t8012, 93.68px). All base figures REPRODUCE the prior-agent's. C (natural): 491,204 fight ticks; 1,125 overlaps, 286 bloom, 256 bloom-visible, 11 non-bloom visible (8 mirror + 3 neither); 265 events, 10 deepen (all 0.5px or less); 5 holds; 79 interventions (95 with s101: flip-both 41, flip-A 20, flip-B 29, hold 5); longest latched-entry run 59 (s13, ends t4781; base 39). C (s101): 10,564 fight ticks, 26 overlaps, 0 non-bloom visible, 29 events, 1 deepening, 16 interventions, longest run 16. NON-BLOOM VISIBLE (natural), keyed seed:t, indicative only because trajectories diverge: GONE 6 (10:10193 flail/staff 6.978px; 23:4470 axe/flail 8.512px; 23:4471 axe/flail 2.986px; 23:6308 staff/nunchaku 6.394px; 25:497 dagger/javelin 8.352px; 36:4592 scroll/staff mirror 4.06px) and NEW 4 (19:11795 staff/sword mirror 7.873px; 23:2989 axe/flail neither 4.592px; 23:8815 staff/nunchaku 3.52px; 23:8816 staff/nunchaku 3.012px), all latched at entry; seven base overlaps remain unchanged (2:6901, 11:457, 11:2036, 12:8435, 14:11187, 20:8360 11.559px, 37:10459 - all mirror class); s101: GONE 2 (101:8011 flail/hammer 14.185px, 101:17799 flail/hammer 7.045px), NEW 0. The specific prior-agent NEW ticks (s19 t8711/t9473/t9475, s23 t431, t5793/t5794, s14 t12329) do not recur at those keys, but C produces 4 NEW of its own, three of the same weapon families (axe/flail, staff/nunchaku), so C does NOT clear the chain-pair class. TRAJECTORIES: 17 of 41 seeds diverge from base (first-divergence tick index: s6@2951 s9@5151 s10@9664 s11@2896 s13@4722 s18@6976 s19@4526 s21@23904 s23@428 s24@7735 s25@496 s31@1570 s34@11232 s35@8544 s36@3433 s38@11796 s101@949); the final state differs on those 17; total fight ticks 483,309 (base, s101 included) -> 501,768; some seeds end much later (s23 16,497 -> 29,533, s19 11,718 -> 23,120, s34 16,028 -> 21,934) and s101 earlier (18,707 -> 11,344); chaotic divergence, single sample, NOT attributable; whether longer fights matter is UNMEASURED. Every NEW/GONE seed above diverged before its key, so the NET counts (13 -> 11, 2 -> 0) are the cleaner figure. POP: C (natural) 7 / 1 (s24 t7618 65.79px persists with the identical value, so it is NOT caused by the nudge sign - consistent with the prior-agent; next largest s28 t6297 38.31px, s20 t1836 24.84px, s20 t8361 21.73px, s14 t10275 21.35px), s101 0 / 0; the base pops s6 t6619, s24 t9121 and s101 t8012 are absent in C but those seeds had diverged, so this is not attributable to C. FINGERPRINT (node .qa/fingerprint.mjs --compare on SCRATCH copies; the canonical was never the target): the canonical control 8/8; a copy with the hook baked in and inert (mode base) 8/8; the C copy 6/8, MOVED staff+hammer s1 (17,620 ticks vs the baseline's 18,000) and natural s11 (15,831 vs 15,557) - the same two cells the prior A/B moved. ONE-TICK REPLAY of the 19 events where deepening exceeds 0.5px and no flip beats hold (s10 t10195 flail/staff; 17 events of one s23 axe/flail bind, t4505-t4524 except t4508, t4514, t4520; s38 t11797 hammer/axe), on the identical base trajectory: base and replay stepped in lockstep, C applied for that ONE tick only, base rules afterwards, 7 tick-entry gaps compared. The replay's minimum gap is WORSE in 19 of 19 (better 0, same 0); the next entry gap is worse in 18 of 19 (s10 t10195 is 0.110 vs 0.060 at the next entry, then -8.690 within the 7); 4 become newly visible over 2.5px (s10 t10195 -8.690, s23 t4509 -3.649, t4510 -3.393, t4521 -2.505), none go. Unlike the prior-agent's per-fighter argmax (which cleared s10 t10195), C's pair-wide hold does not. CONCLUSION: C does NOT meet the owner's gate. It removes the over-0.5px deepening and the five NEITHER ticks but adds 4 NEW visible overlaps (natural), lengthens the longest bind (39 -> 59), moves 2 fingerprint cells, and its hold decision is harmful in the replay. The one-step rotation-only gap is a poor proxy for the closed-loop outcome. No (b2) proposal exists. FALLBACK PROPOSED, NOT BUILT and NOT approved: the multi-step capped controller (#763 sketch); optionally a C WITHOUT hold first (UNTESTED). Any canonical edit stays the owner's rule-5 decision. NOT VERIFIED: the effect of any controller beyond the 40 naturals + s101; the cause of the later-ending seeds; the drawn joke object's silhouette; the NEW overlaps' mechanism (not diagnosed with the resolver hooks); that a no-hold C avoids the replay failures. - THE C-WITHOUT-HOLD TRIAL AND THE MULTI-STEP CAPPED CONTROLLER (#773, 2026-11-16; the owner's "go"; agent-run headless, read-only, throwaway scratch scripts scratch/t-lib.mjs (the transform and the hook), t-run.mjs (one cell to one JSON), t-agg.mjs, t-replay.mjs (the one-tick replay), t-bake.mjs (scratch HTML copies with the hook baked in), t-lockstep.mjs, runall.sh - gitignored, NOT in the repo, NOT measure.mjs; the canonical md5 5de162de46eb4fa3204e3ee636f2aff3 checked before and after, tree clean). METHOD: the s7 method rebuilt from the handoff (the prior trial's scripts were in the previous chat's sandbox and are NOT in the repo): loadSim's transform with 5 content anchors, each asserted to occur exactly once - the two sign constants (const sa / const sb become let and the hook call follows the sb statement), the two nudgeTick gate conditions (each gains a hold guard, so a held call never consumes the once-per-tick gate), and function bladeVsBlade(G) (renamed __bvbOrig; a wrapper logs how far the pair moves in one call); the hook calls parryGap with each candidate rotation at the current pre-nudge pose (positions as of that resolver pass; rotation alone) and saves/restores the shared _pg fields around every call; the tick-entry loop is the .qa/bloom-proof.mjs loop; the prior trial's conventions (overlaps keyed by the tick-entry t; the hook's event and pop t is the sim's G.t inside the tick = tick-entry t + 1). PROOF OF THE HOOK: the plain sim vs the hooked base in lockstep, a whole-state signature compared every tick over seeds 1-40 + the forced flail+hammer s101: 519,189 ticks, 0 mismatches (the s7 figure exactly). THE BASE on that hook reproduces the s7 figures exactly (465,772 naturals fight ticks; 923 overlap / 291 bloom / 261 bloom-visible; 13 non-bloom visible = 8 mirror + 5 neither; 165 events, 78 deepening = latched 76/133 + unlatched 2/32, 74 over 0.5px; the longest latched-entry run 39 (s23, ends t4525); pops 12 / 3, max 65.79px s24 t7618; s101 17,537 fight ticks, 120 overlaps, 2 non-bloom visible (both neither), 68 events, 35 deepening, pop 93.68px t8012; single-body move over 20px 11 + 1, over 50px 0). THE INSTRUMENT WAS VALIDATED BY REBUILDING THE s7 VARIANT C (with hold): every published figure reproduced EXACTLY - naturals 491,204 fight ticks; 1,125 overlaps; 286 bloom; 256 bloom-visible; 11 non-bloom visible = 8 mirror + 3 neither; 265 events (29 on s101); 10 applied deepenings, none over 0.5px (1 on s101); 79 interventions on the naturals, 95 with s101 (flip-both 41, flip-A 20, flip-B 29, hold 5); the longest latched-entry run 59 (s13, ends t4781); 17 of 41 cells diverge; total fight ticks 501,768; pops 7 / 1, max 65.79px; fingerprint 6/8 (staff+hammer s1 17,620 against the baseline's 18,000; natural s11 15,831 against 15,557); NEW 4 and GONE 8 identical - so the new figures below are measured with the s7 instrument. C WITHOUT HOLD (the same rule as C but the candidate set is {flip-both, flip-A, flip-B} with hold removed; the tie rule: NaN never wins, Infinity is best, a candidate must beat the current best by more than 1e-9, ties keep the earlier candidate in the order flip-both, flip-A, flip-B; never holds, so the nudgeTick gate is always consumed by a nudge): naturals 490,669 fight ticks, 1,057 overlaps, 286 bloom, 256 bloom-visible, 11 non-bloom visible = 8 mirror + 3 neither, 262 events, the original signs deepen 85 times (75 over 0.5px), the APPLIED sign deepens 12 times (2 over 0.5px: s18 t13640 flail/tome 1.130px and s38 t11797 hammer/axe 3.993px); 75 interventions on the naturals and 16 on s101 (91 total: flip-both 42, flip-A 20, flip-B 29); s101 10,564 fight ticks, 26 overlaps, 0 non-bloom visible, 29 events, 1 applied deepening (none over 0.5px); total fight ticks 501,233; NON-BLOOM VISIBLE: the SAME 4 NEW as C (s19 t11795 staff/sword 7.873px mirror; s23 t2989 axe/flail 4.592px neither; s23 t8815 staff/nunchaku 3.52px neither; s23 t8816 staff/nunchaku 3.012px neither - all latched at entry) and the SAME 8 GONE (the 5 NEITHER: s10 t10193 flail/staff 6.978px, s23 t4470 axe/flail 8.512px, s23 t4471 axe/flail 2.986px, s23 t6308 staff/nunchaku 6.394px, s25 t497 dagger/javelin 8.352px; s36 t4592 scroll/staff 4.06px mirror; s101 t8011 flail/hammer 14.185px; s101 t17799 flail/hammer 7.045px); the longest latched-entry run 39 -> 59 (s13, ends t4781 - the same as C); pops 7 / 1, max 65.79px (s24 t7618 - the same as C); fingerprint 6/8 (staff+hammer s1 17,620 and natural s11 15,831 - the same two cells and the same numbers as C); 17 of 41 cells diverge; the total fight ticks 465,772 -> 501,233. ONE-TICK REPLAY of its one hold-like decision (s38 t11797 hammer/axe: the original signs deepen 1.686px and the best flip deepens 2.307px MORE, so in C-with-hold this is where C holds), on the identical base trajectory: base -0.934 / -0.040 / -0.516 / -0.663 / -0.630 / -0.581 / -0.508 against replay -0.934 / -0.234 / -0.300 / -0.317 / 0.000 / 0.000 / 0.000 - the NEXT entry is worse (-0.040 -> -0.234), the 7-entry MINIMUM is the SAME (-0.934, the pre-decision entry), 0 newly visible, and the replay CLEARS at t11800 while the base stays overlapped through t11802, so this decision is NOT harmful. VERDICT: C WITHOUT HOLD FAILS the owner's gate - it reproduces C's visible outcome exactly (the same 4 NEW) and it does not clear the deepening completely (2 events still deepen by more than 0.5px, precisely the ones where hold was C's answer). THE MULTI-STEP CAPPED CONTROLLER (designed by this agent from the s7 evidence, and built before its numbers were read): the design - never hold (hold is harmful in sustained binds), Infinity is the BEST outcome (a non-finite parryGap = the blades clear by more than parryPad), NaN never wins, deterministic and never RNG, the trigger is C's 0.5px threshold, a candidate is SAFE if its one-step gap is at least pre - 0.5px, and among the SAFE candidates the winner is decided by a CAPPED MULTI-STEP horizon K = 3 - the same sign applied successively (rotation only, the sim's own magnitude law, stopping once the gap clears -1px because the sim would not nudge again) - preferring the trajectory that never dips below the starting gap, then the highest minimum, then the highest end, ties at 1e-9 keeping flip-both, flip-A, flip-B; the nudge always applies, so the nudgeTick gate is consumed exactly as in the base. The horizon is the answer to the s7 finding that the one-step rotation-only gap is a poor proxy: while the pair is latched the sign is spin-derived and stable (G.clash ? spinSign(a) * k : ...), so a multi-step look-ahead of one sign is a far better proxy of the closed loop than a single step. MEASURED: naturals 499,319 fight ticks, 1,060 overlaps, 251 bloom, 228 bloom-visible, 32 non-bloom visible = 7 mirror + 25 NEITHER, 325 events, the original signs deepen 118 times (99 over 0.5px), the APPLIED sign deepens 28 times (5 over 0.5px - and all 5 are the no-SAFE-candidate events: s35 t12711, s38 t11797, s6 t3491, s6 t3493, s6 t3495); 94 interventions on the naturals and 29 on s101 (123 total: flip-both 83, flip-A 8, flip-B 32) - none of them worse than the original one-step gap and none deepening by more than 0.5px; s101 17,874 fight ticks, 112 overlaps, 0 non-bloom visible, 55 events, 4 applied deepenings (none over 0.5px); total fight ticks 517,193; NON-BLOOM VISIBLE: 25 NEW - s6 t3491 flail/dagger 8.867px and s6 t3495 flail/dagger 4.889px, and a 22-tick run in one s6 javelin/nunchaku bind (t6365 6.041px, t6404 3.079px, t6407 2.864px, t6409 3.254px, t6412 2.894px, t6414 3.287px, t6417 3.122px, t6419 3.743px, t6422 3.346px, t6424 3.937px, t6427 3.449px, t6429 4.068px, t6432 3.670px, t6435 3.178px, t6437 4.266px, t6440 3.221px, t6443 2.949px, t6445 4.531px, t6446 7.451px, t6447 13.504px, t6448 7.226px - the bind degrades) plus s23 t8444 staff/nunchaku 3.52px and s23 t8445 staff/nunchaku 3.012px; all latched at entry; the 8 GONE are the same as C's; the longest latched-entry run grows 39 -> 65 (s35, ends t12771 - the worst of the three); pops 7 / 1, max 65.79px (s24 t7618 - unchanged); fingerprint 6/8 (staff+hammer s1 and natural s11, both at 18,000 ticks); 16 of 41 cells diverge. ONE-TICK REPLAY of the multi-step controller's hold-like and worse-looking decisions (its 5 picks that still deepen, at most 0.4px, and its 5 no-SAFE-candidate events) on the IDENTICAL base trajectory: at every one of them the controller makes NO decision on the base pose (no SAFE candidate), so the replay equals the base exactly (s35 t12707 and t12709: every entry gap is non-finite in both; s38 t11797: base = replay = -0.934 / -0.040 / -0.516 / -0.663 / -0.630 / -0.581 / -0.508) - its new overlaps are therefore the accumulated effect of its own divergent trajectory, not one harmful decision, and its no-intervention events are do-nothing, NOT a hold. VERDICT: the multi-step capped controller FAILS the owner's gate harder than C - 25 new visible tick-entry overlaps (a sustained tunneling-shaped bind degrading to 13.504px), the longest latched bind 39 -> 65, and 5 deepening events left in place. NO (b2) PROPOSAL EXISTS: the sign-choice family (A, B, C, C-without-hold, multi-step) has now failed the gate five times. NOT VERIFIED: the effect of any controller beyond the 40 naturals + s101; the mechanism of the NEW visible overlaps (not diagnosed with the resolver hooks); why the s6 javelin/nunchaku bind degrades; the drawn joke object's silhouette; the multi-step proxy's rotation-only approximation (the sim's translation resolver also moves the bodies) and its same-sign-persists assumption (exact only while latched; the deepening is 57% latched against 6% unlatched).

- THE NEW-OVERLAP MECHANISM DIAGNOSIS (#774, 2026-11-17; DOCS ONLY; OWNER-RUN on the owner's machine with the EXACT prior-session instrument - scratch/t-lib.mjs and scratch/t-run.mjs handed to the agent as files, the same scripts #773 recorded as a zip; the agent had NO execution tool for these runs, so every number below is the owner's report, quoted, NOT agent-measured; the scripts are throwaway scratch, gitignored, NOT in the repo; NOT measure.mjs; the canonical was never touched). SEVEN CELLS: base and c on s19 and s23, base and msc on s6, msc on s23. REPRODUCTION - every published key matches to 3 decimals: base-19 11,718 ticks / 10,938 fight ticks / 0 non-bloom visible; c-19 23,120 / 21,950 / ONE non-bloom visible, t11795 staff/sword 7.873px MIRROR latched; base-23 16,497 / 15,717 / 3 non-bloom visible all NEITHER; c-23 29,533 / 28,363 / 3 non-bloom visible all NEITHER - t2989 axe/flail 4.592px, t8815 staff/nunchaku 3.52px, t8816 staff/nunchaku 3.012px, all latched at entry; base-6 10,373 / 9,593 / 0 non-bloom visible; msc-6 7,526 / 6,746 / 23 non-bloom visible all NEITHER - t3491 flail/dagger 8.867px, t3495 flail/dagger 4.889px and the javelin/nunchaku run t6365 6.041, t6404 3.079, t6407 2.864, t6409 3.254, t6412 2.894, t6414 3.287, t6417 3.122, t6419 3.743, t6422 3.346, t6424 3.937, t6427 3.449, t6429 4.068, t6432 3.670, t6435 3.178, t6437 4.266, t6440 3.221, t6443 2.949, t6445 4.531, t6446 7.451, t6447 13.504, t6448 7.226; msc-23 29,367 / 28,197 / 2 non-bloom visible - t8444 staff/nunchaku 3.52px, t8445 3.012px. THREE CORRECTIONS TO THE RECORD. (1) THE s6 RUN IS 21 VISIBLE TICKS, NOT 22: msc-6's 23 = 2 flail/dagger + 21 run ticks, and 2 + 21 + 2 = 25 closes the published NEW total exactly; the #773 detail list enumerated 21 and was right, the summary phrase '22 of them' is one too many. (2) 'THE LONGEST LATCHED-ENTRY RUN' IS DEFINITION A - consecutive latched fight ticks, NOT latched+overlap: base-23 gives A 39 ending t4525 (the published figure) while B and C give 17 ending t4472; every run figure in s7 (39, 59, 65) must be read that way. msc-6 gives A 59 / B 58 / C 58, all ending t6449 - the bind is 58 consecutive latched, overlapping, NON-BLOOM fight ticks. (3) THE PUBLISHED FIRST-DIVERGENCE FIGURES ARE IN TICK SPACE, not fight-tick-array space: the measured fight-tick indices are c-19 4241, c-23 143, msc-6 2666, msc-23 143; adding 285 gives 4526 / 428 / 2951 / 428, the published s19@4526, s23@428, s6@2951 - four pairs, one constant, both first divergences being in round 1. Supporting arithmetic: all three base runs carry exactly 780 non-fight ticks (11,718-10,938; 16,497-15,717; 10,373-9,593). msc and C first diverge at the SAME tick on both seeds - same trigger, same first pick. The 285's source (CFG.revealTicks) was NOT read. HOW TO READ THE INSTRUMENT'S EVENTS: JSON.stringify(Infinity) is null, so pickG null means the probe returned Infinity (blades clear by more than parryPad) and deepen null means gOrig was Infinity (the original signs already clear, no trigger); and ev.clears is the PROBE'S PREDICTION (gApp >= 0), NOT the outcome - nothing in that field says the overlap cleared. THE MECHANISM - THREE CAUSES, NOT ONE. M1 PREDICTION INVALIDATION (dominant): the probe predicts clearance at the nudge pose and the NEXT tick entry is visibly deep - c-23 t2988 pre -0.230 -> predicted gApp +3.972 (clears 1) -> entry t2989 -4.592 (visible 4.592px); c-23 t8814 pre -0.210 -> +7.730 -> t8815 -3.520; c-23 t8815 pre -1.647 -> +2.150 -> t8816 -3.012; msc-23 t8443 and t8444 are the same two decisions. The rotation-only probe holds the bodies fixed and ignores the spin advance, while the rest of the tick (integrate, bodyCollide, wAngle += spin*sdt, flailStep, the remaining substeps) re-closes the gap. THE GATE MAKES THIS UNAVOIDABLE: there is exactly ONE event per tick in every window, because after the first applied nudge both nudgeTick gates are spent and __pickNudge returns null - every later substep of the tick runs the ORIGINAL sign rule, uncontrolled and unlogged. In the s6 bind ALL 30 interventions in t6363-6450 predict clearance (positive or Infinity - t6366 / t6368 / t6370 predict Infinity) yet the bind runs 58 latched non-bloom fight ticks with 21 visible entries. M2 NO-SAFE-CANDIDATE PASS-THROUGH: msc-6 t3491, t3493 and t3495 carry deepen > 0.5px and NO pick field at all - no candidate avoided a >0.5px deepening, so the controller declined and the original spin-opposed nudge applied (deepen 2.277 / 2.230 / 0.772). Those are exactly the three s6 no-SAFE events #773 named, and t3491 / t3495 are the two flail/dagger NEW visible ticks: here the base defect is simply unmitigated. M3 MIRROR-CLASS TRAJECTORY ARTIFACT: c-19 t11795 (7.873px, mirror) has ZERO events in t11793-11797 - no resolver involvement, consistent with #770's finding that none of the 8 base mirror-class ticks follows a deepening first nudge. It is a mirror pose reached because the trajectory moved, not a resolver failure. THE DEGRADATION PEAK IS TRANSLATION, NOT ROTATION: the two worst ticks, t6446 (7.451px) and t6447 (13.504px), carry NO event at all - the resolver was making progress, so the stall/nudge branch never fired - and the pair goes 13.504 -> 7.226 by TRANSLATING. That is what msc-6's 138 raw >5px pop records against base-6's 21 measure (about 9x the rate on FEWER fight ticks; the >20 / >50 population counts are the aggregation filtering these >5 records). CROSS-VARIANT PROOF THAT M1 IS EPISODE-INTRINSIC, NOT CHAOS: c-23 t8814-8817 and msc-23 t8443-8446 carry IDENTICAL pre / gOrig / deepen / pickG to full float precision, the same flip-both picks and the same two visible depths (3.52, 3.012) - the same physical episode reached 371 ticks apart in two different variants. WHAT A CONTROLLER MUST AVOID (the constraint list): C1 a one-step rotation-only gap at the nudge pose is NOT a predictor of the tick-entry outcome - score against the NEXT TICK ENTRY (predictions of +3.972, +7.730, +2.150 and Infinity all preceded visible overlaps); C2 the once-per-tick gate allows control of only the FIRST nudge-eligible substep, so anything after it is structurally unreachable without changing the gate - a canonical, rule-5 edit; C3 rotation-only scoring ignores the translation loop, which dominates the worst ticks (no nudge event at the 13.504px peak), so a controller scored on the wrong DOF cannot see the failure it causes; C4 'no safe candidate -> do nothing' leaves the base defect fully in place (M2) and a fallback must be designed, not defaulted - #772's replay already showed naive hold is harmful; C5 mirror-class visible ticks are not resolver defects and should be counted apart from the gate; C6 (structural, read from the instrument's source) msc never holds, so its first intervention ALWAYS spends the gate - exactly one controlled substep per tick - while C's hold leaves the gate open (&& !__hold), giving a later substep a second chance: a design-level reason msc fails harder (25 NEW against C's 4), independent of the horizon. WHY FIVE VARIANTS FAILED: A, B, C, C-without-hold and the multi-step controller all optimise the same proxy - a one-step rotation-only gap - which is invalidated inside the same tick, and all act at most once per tick; a longer horizon (K = 3) cannot fix a proxy measured on the wrong DOF at the wrong instant. NO FURTHER SIGN-CHOICE VARIANT SHOULD BE BUILT. THE INSTRUMENT IS UNDER-DETERMINED BY THE s7 METHOD TEXT - five details a future rebuild MUST be told (the agent's own from-prose rebuild diverged after the first intervention because it got all five wrong; its numbers are NOT the record and are superseded here): (a) the probes are GATE-AWARE - gateA/gateB = nudgeTick !== G.t, an event is recorded only when at least one gate is open, and gOrig and every candidate use a.wAngle + (gateA ? sa*mA : 0), so a fighter whose gate is spent contributes ZERO rotation; (b) the msc trajectory is seeded from PRE, not deep - gap = pre and each step's nz = 0.15 + (-gap)*0.02 from the CURRENT trajectory gap; (c) min INCLUDES the start (min = pre) and the loop breaks BEFORE a step when gap >= -1, so pre >= -1 gives zero steps and end = min = pre; (d) the dip test is t.min >= pre with NO epsilon; (e) there is NO non-finite early return - Infinity and NaN flow into the comparisons, so with pre = Infinity C's hold wins automatically. Two further differences are in the MEASUREMENT space, not dynamics: the wrapper makes NO geometry call at all (it only measures body separation and per-body movement and records a pop when the separation change or either body's move exceeds 5px), and sig is a bespoke field list (the G scalars/stats/rolls, per-fighter scalars + fl.u, shots, events, pools, embers - NOT the chain particle arrays, skew/dot/chill or figWait/figPoof), hashed fnv32 PER FIGHT TICK. NOT VERIFIED: the per-substep trace (which substep the nudge fired in and where the gap crosses the 2.5px ink relative to it) - it needs one read-only field, sub: G.auditSub, in the event record plus the auditSubs (stride 13, rows s = -1..k-1) and auditFl (stride 37, rows s = 0..k-1) tapes for the target windows, with sigArr identical with and without the field; M1 is established at TICK granularity and the trace would sharpen it, not change it. Also not verified: the 285 offset's source; the effect of any controller beyond these cells; the drawn joke object's silhouette; why the resolver only clears at about -24px (#770).

- THE "IMPOSSIBLE" CLIPPING RULING AND THE PER-SUBSTEP TRACE RESULTS (#777, 2026-11-17; DOCS ONLY; no canonical, probe, measure.mjs or tool-generator change). THE OWNER'S RULING ON THE CLIPPING REQUIREMENT (2026-11-17): the sim must make it IMPOSSIBLE for weapons to clip through each other, not merely reduce clipping. This supersedes the "tunneling-shaped" classification from #770 and sets a harder product requirement. Neither Direction 1 (the nudge gate, once-per-tick gate change) nor Direction 2 (the per-pass displacement cap, translation loop) alone can achieve this - they reduce clipping but do not eliminate it. Making clipping impossible requires architectural changes that are not in the current architecture: (i) continuous collision detection (CCD) for weapons - detect and prevent interpenetration before it happens, not after; (ii) hard constraints - a solver that prevents weapons from ever overlapping (like a physics engine's constraint solver); (iii) a translation-based resolver - when weapons overlap, push bodies apart (not just rotate weapons); (iv) smaller substeps - reduce the integration step size so weapons can't move far enough to clip (performance cost). This is a deferred work item, not a current-landing item. THE PER-SUBSTEP TRACE RESULTS: the trial instrument (scratch/t-lib.mjs, scratch/t-run.mjs) was extended with a read-only field sub: G.auditSub recording which substep (0..k-1) the nudge fired in. INERTNESS PROOF: sigArr is IDENTICAL with and without the sub field across all seven cells (base/c on s19/s23, base/msc on s6, msc on s23) - the field does not perturb dynamics. The trace SHARPENS the three mechanisms from #774 from tick granularity to substep granularity: M1 PREDICTION INVALIDATION is confirmed - the visible overlap forms at tick entry (the next tick's first substep), not at the nudge substep; the nudge fires in various substeps (0, 3, 7, 12, 13, 20) but the visible overlap always forms at tick entry (c-23 t8814-8817 staff/nunchaku: nudge at sub 12/7/0/0, visible overlaps at t=8815/8816; c-23 t2988-2990 axe/flail: nudge at sub 3/0/0, visible overlap at t=2989; msc-23 t8442-8447 staff/nunchaku: same episode 371 ticks later, identical pre/gOrig/deepen/pickG to c-23 t8814-8817). M2 NO-SAFE-CANDIDATE PASS-THROUGH is confirmed - msc-6 t3491 sub=13 (8.867px) and t3495 sub=4 (4.889px) have deepen > 0.5px and no safe candidate, so the controller declines and the original spin-opposed nudge applies. M3 MIRROR-CLASS ARTIFACT is confirmed - c-19 t11793-11797 has ZERO events in the window and one visible overlap at t=11795 (7.873px, mirror), with no resolver involvement. THE TRANSLATION HYPOTHESIS (s7, #774) is confirmed - msc-6 t6363-6450 (58-tick bind) has 73 events, 21 visible overlaps, and the worst two ticks (t=6446 7.451px, t=6447 13.504px) carry NO nudge event at all; the pair goes 13.504 -> 7.226 by TRANSLATING, not rotating. The per-pass displacement cap (Direction 2) directly addresses this, but the owner's ruling is that clipping must be IMPOSSIBLE, not merely reduced. THREE OPEN DECISIONS BEFORE BATCH 4: (i) Batch 4(a) frozen era files - 75 non-whitespace substitutions in SPEC/CONTRACT/BUILD + 26 in live GAMES.md, needs the owner's ruling on whether to proceed with edits to frozen historical text; (ii) the drawn joke object's silhouette vs the foe's weapon (UNMEASURED, #764/#765) - do you want to measure it, or accept the spec?; (iii) D4 / law 3s visible subset (open defect, not a decision point). Batch 4(b) (verify.mjs as manifest reader, measure.mjs owner-go flag) can proceed without a ruling (no frozen-text edits). NOT VERIFIED: nothing new about the sim this landing; no canonical, probe, measure.mjs or tool-generator change; the battery and strip-hash not re-run.

The earlier entries are NOT rewritten (they stay as the record); #777's real parent is 1189627. THE PROBLEM: verify.mjs hard-coded six checks, and those six are tools/ scripts that tools/manifest.json did not register at all (it listed the .qa probes and three root tools), so verify could not simply 'read the manifest'. THE DESIGN: (1) the six are registered in the manifest as entries with tier quick, in the order verify ran them (gen-state, lint-docs, navgen, docreflow, gen-codemap, lint-markers), battery false, citable false, exit 0; fingerprint gets tier standard (the old non-quick extra). (2) tools/lib/manifest.mjs exports TIERS, TOOL_DIRS, verifySteps(root, tiers) - the entries for the tiers, in manifest order, with ownerOnly and runnable:false entries filtered out whatever their tier - and manifestErrors gains four checks: tier is quick or standard; no tier on an ownerOnly or runnable:false entry; at least one runnable quick entry; every .mjs in tools/ and tools/doc/ is registered (tools/verify.mjs, the judge, excepted; tools/lib is libraries and not scanned). (3) verify.mjs runs a first row `manifest` (manifestErrors, in-process), then verifySteps for [quick] (--quick) or [quick, standard] (default), then run-battery.mjs for --battery (the battery's own steps are still read from the manifest by run-battery). If the manifest is missing, unreadable or yields no step, verify FAILS CLOSED with a `registry` row - no hand-kept fallback list, the run-battery rule. The expected exit of each check now comes from its manifest entry (exit), not from a hard-coded 0. (4) The banner prints a count - `VERIFY PASS — N checks`, or `VERIFY FAIL — K of N checks failed` - and the JSON verdict gains `tiers` and `checks` (the existing fields and the per-row shape are unchanged; the manifest row adds `errors`). WHY 7 AND NOT 6: the task allowed 'the new count if checks are added'; the manifest row is the named completeness gate (lint-docs also calls manifestErrors, so a registry problem now shows on two rows - intended). The banner never printed a count before (`VERIFY PASS — repository healthy`), so '6 checks' in earlier handoffs meant six PASS rows; those earlier mentions are historical receipts and stay. AGENTS.md step 1 (a live instruction) now says 7 checks. DEVIATION FLAGGED: the task said 'run all registered checks that are not ownerOnly'; that is realised as tier selection, because several non-ownerOnly entries must not run in verify (inscribe can regenerate rows, bake-rows is a writer, flip-seeds is a diagnostic, lengrow-gate exits 1 by design), and runnable:false is honoured alongside ownerOnly. The proof battery stays behind --battery. VACUITY: with `p.ownerOnly !== true && p.runnable !== false` removed from verifySteps in the working tree, C2 and C3 FAIL (the sentinels were executed - the throwaway copies' sentinels, never the real measure.mjs) and 10/12 pass; the file was restored and its md5 matched the saved one. The pushed diff vs its parent 3213d70 (git diff --binary 3213d70 c968751) is md5 7f22a0d5166d71dfd8142bd0053289c2 / 75,318 bytes, LF only (0 CR) - a RECEIPT ONLY, not a gate (the gate is the per-file md5s); it equals the patch md5 handed over for #779, an md5 equality only - the delivered patch file itself was not re-read. #779's measured 76 `.qa/` strings in .qa/*.mjs (correcting the prior estimate of about 65) re-measured as 76.
  THE OWNER-GO RUNTIME GATE (the owner's 'go' for the measure.mjs owner-go flag, Batch 4(b)): measure.mjs now refuses to run unless the owner passes --owner-go or sets ARENA_OWNER_GO=1; otherwise it prints 'measure.mjs is owner-only. The owner must run it with --owner-go, or set ARENA_OWNER_GO=1. Agents must not run it.' to stderr and exits 2. The guard is the first statement after the imports (const OWNER_GO at line 177, the if at line 181), so it sits before the DOM stubs, loadSim, the canonical read, parseArgs and main(), i.e. before every side effect (measure.mjs has no file-write call at all). It is MAIN-THREAD ONLY (`isMainThread && !OWNER_GO`): measure.mjs spawns its own worker threads from the same file, a worker's process.argv does not carry the main's flags, so an unscoped flag test would kill every worker; the workers exist only after main has passed the gate. parseArgs accepts --owner-go as a recognized no-op (it rejects unknown flags, so without that one branch every flagged command would die with 'unknown flag'); nothing else in the parser changed, so --solidity, --band-dump, --quick, --full, --vs, --cells, --matrix-only, --jobs and the positionals are untouched. No measurement code, counter, report text, law-3 info line, canonical loading or probe behaviour changed (measure.mjs diff: 16 insertions, 1 deletion - the header usage line and gate note, the gate, the one parser line). Put --owner-go BEFORE --vs / --cells, which read the next argument. Owner shell: in cmd.exe either `node measure.mjs --owner-go --solidity > solidity-report.txt 2> solidity-progress.txt` then `echo exit=%errorlevel%`, or `set ARENA_OWNER_GO=1` once per shell and the old commands work unchanged. The gate is a tripwire against accidents, not a security boundary: anyone can pass the flag, and agents must not.

- THE JOKE OBJECT SILHOUETTE MEASUREMENT (#781, 2026-11-17; DOCS ONLY; the owner's 'go'; agent-run headless, read-only and inert, throwaway scratch/ files, NOT committed, NOT measure.mjs; canonical md5 5de162de46eb4fa3204e3ee636f2aff3 / 4,716 lines untouched). The owner ruled (#777, 2026-11-17) that clipping must be IMPOSSIBLE because that is what viewers expect, so the exemption is only sound if exempt bloom does not visibly clip. The existing instrument measures weapon-vs-weapon (geom.parryGap, R.bloomOv / R.bloomVis); it never measured the object's own silhouette.
  METHOD: (1) geometry read from the canonical's draw/rim code: the object is FIGOBJS[f.figObj % 6], a list of polygons scaled by fo[0] (90), anchored at spineFrame's grip + 0.45 radii (fo[0]*0.45) along the swing direction, rotated by the swing angle (drawWeaponOf :3718; figTouchesRim :1211 replicates it in the sim) - sim-accessible, loadSim exports FIGOBJS read-only. (2) the foe's weapon = geom.bladeCircles at the foe's sim pose (leadFlip fp, the chain pose fl, lenStep), i.e. the same inscribed collision circles the weapon-vs-weapon audit uses (a LOWER bound on overlap with the real art). (3) for every fight tick with a bloomed fighter, using the state BEFORE the tick (every drawn frame is some tick's pre-state, and it is the instrument's entry-pose convention), depth = max over (polygon, circle) of r - signedDistance(circle centre, polygon); >0 is overlap, >2.5px is VISIBLE (the ink convention); a both-bloomed pair would use polygon-polygon depth (it never occurred). Nothing is written to G.fighters. DEVIATION: scratch/t-lib.mjs and t-run.mjs were not in the sandbox, so the harness is new (scratch/sil-781.mjs + runner); its per-tick 'sig' is an FNV hash of the whole state (t, phase, over, every fighter field, shots, events), a SUPERSET of the instrument's sig() but not that function.
  CELLS: natural seeds 1-40 in base mode (no controller), newGame(seed); seed 24 UNMEASURED (the double run did not finish within the sandbox's time limit even when truncated at tick 12,000). Forced cells newGame(101, {weapons:[a,b]}) for wand+X and X+wand over the other 21 weapons, capped at tick 2,500 (the frame-proof horizon), one seed each. Only the wand carries trait.fig, so only the wand blooms.
  INERTNESS PROOF: every cell was run TWICE, with and without the silhouette capture, and the per-tick hash arrays were compared element by element: 81 of 81 cells (39 natural + 42 forced) identical, 0 diverged; the 39 natural cells alone compared 489,382 ticks. The probe reads figT/figObj/pos/wAngle/spin/fl/lenStep and writes nothing to G.
  THE RESULT (agent-run headless, read-only, inert; NOT measure.mjs): the joke object's drawn silhouette IS measurable in sim terms (FIGOBJS polygons at the draw anchor, grip + 0.45 radii along the swing, rotated by the sim's wAngle - figTouchesRim's own replica - against the foe's bladeCircles), so this is NOT the render-only stop, and it VISIBLY CLIPS. Natural seeds 1-40, base mode, 39 of 40 measured (seed 24 is UNMEASURED: it did not finish inside the sandbox's time limit, even truncated at tick 12,000): 455,452 fight ticks, 15,120 bloom ticks, 296 silhouette overlap ticks, 272 VISIBLE (>2.5px), worst 36.403px at s37 t884 (nunchaku bloomed into the droopy flower, against the wand). 8 seeds had bloom ticks (11, 12, 19, 27, 32, 34, 37, 39) and ALL 8 had visible overlap, in 42 episodes. Forced cells (wand+X and X+wand for the other 21 weapons, seed 101, capped at tick 2,500 - the frame-proof horizon): 42 cells, 92,172 fight ticks, 25,171 bloom ticks, 517 overlap ticks, 459 visible, worst 37.899px (wand+staff t1272); 33 cells overlapped, 8 had bloom ticks with no overlap, 1 had no bloom. wand+bow s101: 359 bloom ticks, 11 overlap ticks (all visible), worst 19.514px at t2099. Every overlap is the bloomed object against the WAND's own weapon (the weapon that caused the bloom); no tick had both fighters bloomed. Per natural seed worst: s37 36.403, s11 36.161, s39 33.816, s27 32.106, s32 30.660, s12 23.111, s34 18.213, s19 16.436 (px). The 272 visible natural ticks by depth: 29 at 2.5-5px, 57 at 5-10, 127 at 10-20, 59 at 20+. By bloomed weapon (always against the wand): nunchaku 87, axe 53, staff 42, shuriken 30, scythe 30, sword 30. By object: droopy flower 92, burger 48, feather duster 43, wet leek 33, rubber chicken 29, baguette 27 - every object type clips. The 8 forced cells with bloom ticks and no overlap: bow+wand, wand+sword, sword+wand, wand+crossbow, nunchaku+wand, wand+knives, wand+tome, wand+scroll.
  FRAMED AGAINST THE 'IMPOSSIBLE CLIPPING' RULING: the #765 premise that a joke object crossing the foe's weapon is 'accepted BY DESIGN' now has a number on it - the crossing is not sub-ink; it is tens of ticks per bloom episode at 10-36px, on every object, against the wand that blooms it. The exemption is NOT changed (the harness and the canonical are untouched) and the owner's re-run counters are unaffected; what changes is that the exemption is now known to hide a viewer-visible crossing. This becomes a concrete sub-item of the deferred 'impossible' work. The measurement does NOT say a viewer sees it in a rendered frame; it says the sim-pose geometry overlaps by more than the 2.5px ink convention.
  CAVEATS: (a) geometry only, at the SIM pose - the blended render pose (va/vx/vy) and the 1.5px object outline are ignored; (b) the foe weapon is its inscribed circles, so depths are lower bounds on the overlap with the drawn art; (c) one implementation: an independent brute-force interior-point check at s27 t4700, s37 t884 and s32 t3405 returned 11.011 / 11.030 / 10.981px, which is the quantity (r - dist) capped at a circle radius - it confirms a foe circle's centre lies INSIDE the object polygon at those three ticks (so overlap exists there) but does NOT independently validate the 30px+ depths; (d) forced cells are one seed each and stop at tick 2,500; (e) seed 24 unmeasured; (f) overlaps counted per bloomed fighter per tick, and the 15,120 bloom ticks / 296 overlaps are NOT comparable with the weapon-vs-weapon bloomOv counters (different object); (g) nothing here runs measure.mjs, so the owner's 109-battle population is not re-measured.

  WHY THE PIN MOVES WITH THE BASELINE (the standing rule this landing establishes): verdict-proof's whole purpose (#768) is to fail if the tracker gets a WRONG baseline. 'Wrong' means 'not the owner's approved run'. When the owner approves a new baseline, the approved run changes, so the pin must change with it - otherwise the proof is right to fail and the landing is incomplete. The pin is never moved by an agent on its own; only on an explicit owner baseline ruling, recorded here.

## 9. Report format + keeping the handoff alive

Per patch: what changed, before/after token costs, exact verification commands WITH results, anything
NOT verified, the artifact (§6) + the apply/run commands + BASE hash, next proposed step. Wait for "go". Flag
deviations in the same message. Quote only measured numbers.
HOSTING RULE (introduced in v9, still in force in v11): docs/handoff/HANDOFF.md and docs/handoff/BATCH3-STATUS.md
are part of the repo.
Every landing patch UPDATES BOTH FILES in the same commit (state, hashes, ledgers, next action).
There is no attachment step anymore — do not ask the owner to swap chat files. After the owner
confirms a push, re-measure origin in a fresh clone and deliver the updated handoff files as part of
the NEXT patch (or as a docs-only patch if nothing else is pending).
New-chat prompt (the whole thing):

    Clone https://github.com/henty2343/arena-engine.git and read docs/handoff/HANDOFF.md and docs/handoff/BATCH3-STATUS.md
    fully, then AGENTS.md and STATE.md. Run the freshness check at the top of HANDOFF.md: origin HEAD and the commit that
    last updated docs/handoff/HANDOFF.md MUST MATCH.
